I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving to five. Agenda item — Proposal for a law supplementing the law restricting the use of tobacco and related products, reduced procedure. The draft law was tabled by a group of 23 Members and Members with the first-signed Mr Saš Tabaković. In order to further explain the bill, I give the floor to the representative of the motioners, Mr Sasa Tabakovic.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u2	Dear colleagues and colleagues, representatives of the Ministry, good morning! Today, we are considering the Law on the Limitation of the Use of Tobacco and Related Products, which would allow herbal cigarette substitutes to be used for their artistic expression and production only on stage during the performance. Several members of both the coalition and the opposition have assessed that in the case of theatres at the last ZOUTPI novelty, when the ban on the use of herbal cigarettes in closed rooms was equated with a normal cigarette, it was overly restrictive to the field of artistic activity – we have already warned about this when the last ZOUTPI was adopted. It should be borne in mind that much of the Slovenian and world dramas of the 19th or 20th century, which Slovenian theatres are obliged to carry out under the constitution, could not be properly included in the repertoires of the season. Some theatres have also announced the withdrawal, some have already withdrawn certain performances from the current repertoire, as direct intervention or change under the current law would not be possible because dramatic works from this period simply stem from the time of society when smoking was an integral part of life or social patterns when awareness towards the harmful consequences of nicotine use has not yet been spread. Moreover, at certain social moments, an important part of the emancipation of social groups, a description of the situation and, in particular, of the individual's personality characteristics, followed, of course, by dramatic proposals. And the artist on stage only further underlines this situation. Therefore, smoking cigarette substitutes in these performances is a dramatic necessity and not a whim of artistic creators, as it has been heard several times by certain civil society. The use of other substitutes also depends on the aesthetics of the show itself, which has already been explained on the parent committee. Let me say that since 2007 when ZOUTPI was adopted, no smoking has been allowed in the theatre as a public space. The exception is the stage where they were used for the artistic production of herbal cigarettes. In this way, the repertoire choice of theatres did not collapse, but at the same time the community's decision to reduce tobacco consumption in public places was overly respected. With the amendment of the law, we are in fact merely preserving the situation in 2007, which in terms of proportionality has proved to be the need for artistic creation to be somehow optimal and, above all, to be the most appropriate in practice. I would like to point out that no one on the show is forcing the use of cigarette substitutes. The audience was also well informed about the performance on the stage of the incense before entering the hall. With this exception, we are not unique in Slovenia, including other European countries, some are listed in the bill itself, for artistic reasons despite the implementation of the European directive, which is the basis of the tobacco law, allowing the use of cigarette substitutes. Let me end by adding that it is true that everyone has the right to a healthy environment, and the fact is that no one objects. Nor are the signatories of this novel, but the theatre with all the elements that connect it is in the first place a space of social consensus between the performer and the audience. Keep it that way for the future, so on behalf of all the signatories, I ask you for your support. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u3	Thank you very much. The draft law was treated by the Health Committee as a working mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to Vice-President Branko Zorman. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u4	Dear Mr President, distinguished Secretary of State, colleague and colleague! The report of the Health Committee is as follows. The Health Committee is at its 10th meeting. 5. 2017 as a parent working body, the proposal for a law supplementing the Law on the Limitation of the Use of Tobacco and Related Products, submitted by a group of Members and Members with the first signed Saša Tabakovic to the National Assembly for consideration and adoption following a reduced procedure. The College of the President of the National Assembly shall be held at its 91st meeting, on 13. 4. 2017 decided that the proposal for a law should be examined in a simplified manner. Member Saš Tabakovic's amendment to Article 1 has been tabled to the draft law within a set time limit. The introductory explanation of the bill was given by Saša Tabaković. He stressed that the aim of the proposed amendment was to enable the realization of the constitutionally protected right to free artistic creation and expression and artistic freedom. The proposed amendment allows smoking in closed areas, which are intended for cultural activities by means of herbal products for smoking if smoking is part of the stage art play. The stage art is a common title for the artistic expression for speech, gesture, sound, movement with which the artist presents himself in the performance and includes ballet, dance, opera, pantomime and play. Smoking is allowed only on stage and during the duration of the performance. Only smoking of herbal products that do not contain tobacco or nicotine and which have no harmful effects on the health of both performers and viewers is permitted. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service presented their written legal opinion and said that freedom of artistic creation, as provided for in Article 59 of the Constitution, is a special form of freedom of expression provided for in Article 39 of the Constitution. The constitutional provision contains a valuable principle and undertakes to maintain and promote artistic creation without any legal restriction, but this does not mean that freedom of artistic creation is totally unlimited. The restrictions laid down by the Constitution are permissible alone and the protection of the rights of others. The opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia was presented by the State Secretary of the Ministry of Health. It stressed that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia disagrees with the proposal for a reduction in the harmfulness of herbal cigarettes that do not contain tobacco. The government also points out that, in addition to the right to freedom of artistic creation, the right to a healthy living environment and the equal protection of fundamental rights and freedoms and equality from the law are protected by the Constitution. The opinion of the National Council of the Commission for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons was presented by mag. Peter Pozun. He stressed that the Commission does not support the bill and notes that NGOs are also opposed to the proposed amendments. Hereinafter, representatives of NGOs and the National Institute for Public Health presented their views on the draft law. In the course of the discussion, the applicant rejected the allegations of paid advertising by the tobacco industry issued by representatives of non-governmental organisations. Support was expressed for the bill by members of the committee. The Committee voted on the amendment to Article 1 and adopted it. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on the articles of the bill and adopted them.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u5	Thank you very much. For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government of Mrs Jožica Maučec Zakotnik, State Secretary at the Ministry of Health. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u6	Thank you very much, Mr. Chairman. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! The proposed amendment to the Tobacco and Related Products Restriction Act provides an exception to the prohibition on smoking in all closed public and labour areas. It allows smoking of herbal products for smoking on stage during the duration of the performance in closed public spaces, which are intended for cultural activities, if smoking is part of the stage art play. The main objective of the bill is, according to the proposaler, to enable the exercise of the constitutionally protected right to free artistic creation. The Ministry of Health considers that the law in force does not constitute a restriction on artistic freedom, as there are props on the market for use in theatres. With the use of these props, it is possible to show smoking in performances. That is the case with cases of countries where smoking of tobacco and herbal products on stage such as Austria, Finland, Scotland and Wales is also prohibited. The amendment of the law within such a short period of time after its entry into force is an undue pressure on the revision of the regime, which was adopted by the National Assembly without a vote. In practice, legal solutions have not yet been fully implemented and therefore it is not possible to assess their appropriateness or effectiveness. This is also recalled by the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, which, in its opinion, stated that the applicant does not explain in his explanation why it is not possible to illustrate smoking through props and thus ensure both free artistic expression and a healthy environment. In addition, we stress that the current regulation has high public support, as a recent public opinion survey has shown that more than two-thirds of the respondents support the law, and only 20% support it. The theatres, in accordance with the previous law, did not have any specific exceptions which would allow the use of herbal products for smoking as claimed by the proposer. The 2007 Act dealt only with tobacco products and herbal products were not included in the provisions of the law at all. Today, we know that smoking herbal cigarettes or exposure to the smoke of herbal cigarettes is equally harmful to health. The smoke of herbal cigarettes is similar to that of carbon monoxide, tar and a number of carcinogenic and health-related substances. We note that in addition to the right to freedom of artistic creation, the right to a healthy living environment is protected by the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia and the equal protection of fundamental rights and freedoms and equality from the law. Players, like all other employees, have the right to a healthy working environment in which they are not exposed to the harmful effects of tobacco and related products. The proposed amendment interferes with the regulation of tobacco control in such a way that a particular group of employees reduces the right to smoke unpolluted air with tobacco and even forces it to smoke harmful herbal cigarette substitutes. In its opinion, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia pointed out the harmful effects of the use of herbal cigarettes and stressed that, in addition to the right to free artistic creation, the right to a healthy living environment is protected by the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. Nor did the competent commission of the Council of State support the proposed amendments to the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u10	Thank you for your word, chairman. In the United Left, we support the proposed amendment to the tobacco law. In some respects, however, the law intervened in the freedom of artistic creation, which we in the United Left had already pointed out during its adoption and put in place appropriate amendments, but unfortunately they were rejected two months ago. Today's proposal, which has also received support on the parent's working body, allows players to use herbal substitutes to stage characters that require smoking. In this context, the proposal specifies that the exception applies only to theatre, it is said, to premises intended exclusively for cultural activities and only for players during the performance. The proposal protects the constitutionally protected right to free artistic creation, without jeopardising the legitimate aims of banning smoking and advertising cigarettes. Smoking within the stage art is not performed in the role of the promotion of tobacco products, but art has its own function and meaning. It can serve as a presentation of the character, showcases his class membership, presents his social habits, and portrays the life of a certain time. This law proposes that the long-standing practice of the use of cigarette substitutes in theatre performances be maintained, as it is an important element for the implementation of performances which does not undermine the purpose of the original law. I would like to point out that the law prohibits so-called indirect advertising in its 29th Article, which means that the player must not display a pack of cigarettes with the label of the manufacturer. In the United Left, we supported new tobacco legislation mainly because it stepped on the fingers of large tobacco multinationals and limited their profits for the benefit of public health. In doing so, we have noted that the problem of addiction is largely due to social conditions and social relations. People don't die for cigarettes as much as they die for social exclusion and poverty. And we can't put anything like that on a theater show. In the United Left, we point out that over-prohibition and moralisation can discredit such measures to improve public health and achieve exactly the opposite effects. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u13	The word shall be given by the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, Mrs Janja Slug to her.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u14	Thank you very much. The purpose of the amendment to the Act on the Limitation of the Use of Tobacco and Related Products is to enable the exercise of the constitutionally protected right to free artistic creation and the application of the exception already known or permitted by the original law from 1996 to 2007. Since 2007, there has been a total ban on tobacco use in theatres and the use of so-called substitute herbal cigarettes has been permitted. The law did not then address herbal and other related products. The view prevailed that, for example, in theatres and other performances of stage art, smoking cigarettes, pipe and cigar is an integral part of artistic expression. Therefore, smoking of tobacco products was prohibited in theatres, except for the purposes of performances, where the performing parties could use substitutes for tobacco products, while the theatres adequately equipped the premises in which they were performing for smoke performances and the new law also prohibited the use of such smoke substitutes for smoke. The proposed amendment allows for an exception to smoking in closed areas, namely, smoking of herbal products in closed public areas which are intended for cultural activities. We do not oppose the new law, but we have supported it not least, but we note that, in addressing it, there has been a disregard for the exception mentioned above and that the restrictions, which, by reason of public interest in the field of health, may affect the communication value of works of art. While we did not want to raise the latter in the debate because of the multitude of more important issues, we did not want to make it difficult to adopt a law that was already under strong pressure. The amendment adds a new Article 39a to the text of the current law, which introduces exceptions to smoking in closed spaces. In accordance with the principle of proportionality, they would only allow smoking of herbal products, i.e. suitable substitutes for tobacco products that do not contain tobacco or nicotine and do not have such harmful effects on the health of actors and viewers. However, the exception, in accordance with the principle of proportionality, is in a narrower sense limited only to the stage and only to the duration of the performance. This exception will not apply to exercises. In performances of stage art, smoking can be part of the author's expression and thus the prohibition would extend to the very essence or integrity of artistic work. If the law in force were to be applied in an unaltered form, they should interfere with the works of art or their message value. The professional public notes, however, that Slovenian theatres present a major part of the classical parts of the previous century, when awareness towards the harmful effects of nicotine use was no enlargement and that the ban on smoking on theatre stage would significantly impoverish repertoires, since by erasing parts of scenes in some parts they would trim or destroy the components of the author's work. It is worth noting that the proposed regulation is comparable to those in some other Member States of the European Union, which, in spite of restrictive art legislation, allow for exceptions; it allows smoking of tobacco products or substitutes. It is worth noting that the amendment does not violate a prohibition restricting the advertising of tobacco and tobacco products, as works of art are not intended to promote smoking. For example, many dramas or scenes are often linked to smoking because they describe social habits at the time and so forth. For in stage art life is depicted as it is, and part of it is smoking. Allow me to use the debate of one of my fellow Members, my colleague Ivan Prelog, on the committee that supported the novel with the following words: "In defence, I must express to my colleague Mr Tabakovic the fear that such or such exaggerations will lead to outrageous absurd absurdities that will make our lives worse. I bet on a normal life myself, and I'm sure I'm not the only one.” We will therefore support the proposal in the SMC Group. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u20	Dear Mr President of the National Assembly, honourable Members, honourable Members! You have before you the proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Health Act, which will make an important contribution in the field of health to the preservation of the social state. Why? Because with it we maintain a solidarity public health care. This means that our health is based on a network of public health institutions and concessionaries or private individuals enter the public system where they need a doctor and the public network does not provide it. I must point out that this law does not specifically regulate the status of private doctors for whom citizens and citizens pay for their services. A number of ministers have failed to attempt to amend the Health Act. That is why I am particularly pleased that my team and this government and the Ministry have succeeded in bringing the law before you, Members and Members of Parliament, to the National Assembly, where we have the opportunity to explain what the law actually brings. Because the opposition and criticisms of the law were very intense and often unjustifiable, sometimes misleading, especially when those who disagreed with the legal solutions tried to show the public that the adoption of the law meant a breakdown of the health system. On the contrary! This law is a health law for people. Not only because Slovenians put health first, but mainly because health in Slovenia remains public, accessible to all people, regardless of the level of income. The proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Health Act regulates the organisation and implementation of health activities in the Republic of Slovenia, which has not been amended for 25 years, that is, since the adoption of the law, and our country and we have changed significantly during this time. What does marriage do? First, it determines who and under what conditions in our country may be authorised to carry out medical activities. It further specifies when concessions can be granted. Again, after the new award of the concession, it will not depend on the wishes of private doctors, as it has been so far, but on the needs of the people of Slovenia. Furthermore, the law regulates the health activity of certain social and educational institutions which have been released in the current law. It introduces different forms of control over all healthcare providers, which has not been regulated either. Finally, it regulates what forms of advertising of health activities and health services are allowed in the Republic of Slovenia; let me just remind you how many misleading ads there have been. Dear Members and Members! I am confident that the proposed changes for patients will mean greater quality and safety of health services and that our public health system will be more transparent and that people will have access to a doctor in public health institutions. Allow me to point out the case of Austria, which is much ahead of Slovenia, but we overstepped them when we had access to a personal physician. It was precisely for these reasons that the Government insisted on this law, although there have been many objections since the moment we presented the proposal. He was also part of the professional public and some medical organizations. One of their main findings was that we favour public health institutions in relation to private people in obtaining the right to provide health services from public funds. That is why I repeat again – from public funds. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u25	The text is addressed to Mr Branko Zorman, Group of the Modern Centre Party.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u26	Dear Mr President, representatives of the Government, colleagues, colleagues! The proposal for a novel of the Health Act has been subject to broad and long-term coordination. The primary objective of these changes is to maintain and strengthen a health system based on public health in a way that strengthens the public health service and brings about changes that improve the implementation of health activities in order to ensure greater transparency, efficiency, expertise and more effective supervision. The novelty removes the subordination and weakness of the current system in the field of authorisation for the exercise of health activities. By laying down the conditions and procedures for the authorisation and the precise conditions for the withdrawal of licences, for the first time all health service providers will be treated equally. The conditions for obtaining a permit will apply equally to all private healthcare professionals and private doctors who are authorised to carry out medical activities by entering a register of private health professionals or a register of doctors. One of the main and public solutions concerns the area of awarding concessions. The bill follows the objective of strengthening public health in a way that establishes a system that will allow for the award of concessions in a transparent and uniform manner. Concessionaires are still an important part of the public health network following this proposal, as they complement this network in cases where public health institutions cannot or cannot carry out certain health services to the extent necessary. Strategic planning is envisaged both at the primary and secondary levels, as the basis for the tendering of the concession is a prior act of concession, which must include, inter alia, an explanation and a determination of the conditions for the tendering of the concession. This ensures the granting of concessions for the execution of health activities in a transparent and competitive manner, which are two basic principles of concessionary relations. It is also complemented by the provision of conditions for the provision of health services to healthcare professionals employed in public health establishments outside the public service. It is laid down in the general rule that a healthcare professional employed in a public health service or another public service undertaking carrying out a health activity may provide health services to another healthcare professional only under specified conditions. The terms and conditions vary according to whether they are the provision of health services to another public institution, i.e. the public health institution or another public institution, or to another health operator. The diversity of regulation stems from the existence of elements of the competitiveness of activities and the existence of a risk of conflicts of interest and corruption risks, which we are well aware of from existing practices of the health system. The proposal also comprehensively regulates all forms of supervision of healthcare providers, inspection of the implementation of the law and the area of advertising of health services, which has not been regulated so far. With the amendment to the Health Act, we follow the coalition objective of strengthening public health in order to better separate public and private health activities. Quality and effective health care is still our priority, as we believe that the health system must maintain its universality and accessibility, which can only be ensured through a clearer border between public and private health activities. The bill brings these important steps towards strengthening public health, which is why we support it in the Parliamentary Group of the Modern Centre Party. More about the content itself then during the debate.

We note that the Ministry of Health has chosen, as its objective and priority, areas that are not essential for tackling the accumulated problems in the public system. We would be pleased if today, after more than two years and after an analysis by experts from the World Health Organisation, we could consider a comprehensive new proposal from the Health Act and not just some amendments and additions to the existing law, while the key areas remain unchanged for the time being. Of course, we can agree that it is reasonable to regulate the award of concessions and welcome the regulation of advertising in the field of health. Monitoring the work of health workers itself is also useful to upgrade with the possibility of systematic supervision, but the proposal itself does not solve the problem of ineffective control. Unfortunately, in this part too, the bill is too ambitious. Even more problems in this bill are the exchange between public and private health, which still does not follow all the objectives of the coalition contract and the regulation of the work of doctors outside public health institutions. We also committed ourselves in the coalition contract to ensuring an equal position between public and private health operators. In view of the boisterous response from the medical species, we fear that by adopting the proposed solutions in the novel, we are primarily opening up new possibilities for legal disputes and exacerbation, which will certainly not be of benefit to patients. In doing so, we believe that the Ministry of Health has ensured that the law is not contrary to European Community law. It is also serious to note that, despite the emphasis on public health care, some solutions can be misleading the very foundations of the system, which I believe none of us want. It is the responsibility of the Minister and the competent Ministry of Health to provide the proposed and perhaps insufficient solution and to insist on them. In the Desus Group, we believe that the proposed amendment to the Health Act does not constitute the necessary reform changes, but it removes some inconsistencies in the existing regime. We can be satisfied with the proposal that eliminates injustices at the expense of dividing work between middle- and graduate nurses. For too long after the adoption of the flawed Law on the Pay of Public Servants, those experienced midwives who were competent in the workplaces of qualified nurses were severely deprived. This is also one of the more important reasons why the proposal for the amendment of the Health Act in the first reading will be largely supported.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u32	Thank you so much for saying that. All of you, once again, have a nice hello! The expectations of the Social Democrats regarding this law have been known to the general public for some time. We have written in our programming documents that we want a clear distinction between public and private implementation of health activities, but until this can be achieved, we want to stop the issue of concessions and the transfer of concessions from natural persons to legal persons or parts. Thus, at our request in the coalition agreement, we have written down that we will prepare a new Health Action Act, because, inter alia, we want to distinguish publicly from private health activities, set clear criteria for the public health sector network and regulate the area of award of concessions in a way that we will only award them on the basis of a fixed-term tender where public health institutions cannot provide the intended scope of services. It should be pointed out that today's proposal only partially follows a coalition agreement, since our commitment was to prepare a new Health Action Act, not just amendments and additions, that changes to the law would be based on an assessment of the situation and the public health service network. This proposal only touches on the areas of awarding concessions and, for us, on the arrangements for the status of nurses. Allow me, however, to touch upon the award of concessions in our presentation. In awarding concessions, all of us find that it is unplanned, on the basis of desire, but not on the basis of health needs and demographic changes of the population. It has also been possible to transfer concessions from natural persons to legal persons so far, and this is a phenomenon where concessions have been granted to individual doctors for an indefinite period or for a period of time when they can carry out this activity independently. Now, some concessionaires want to transfer concessions to legal persons, the so-called parts that they have set up themselves. Such an indefinite concession is virtually impossible after the current law and, together with the company, it can be sold, inherited, beyond all possible criteria. This is unacceptable in our view. The new Health Act, which is now in first reading, has been in public debate for a long time, where everyone has had the opportunity to comment. Much of the law is so technical in nature and talks about procedures, but substantive changes are not so much. As I mentioned, the area of healthcare concessions is currently poorly regulated, leading to different practices in the award of concessions. The current system allows for significant corruption risks, as there are no criteria, when and under what conditions a concession can be granted, so it is possible to get the impression that concessions are granted on the basis of friendly and other connections and not the actual needs of an increasingly ageing population. This should no longer be possible after the adoption of this law. What will this be like, the practice will show itself. Why is that? Because we still don't have a public health service network. However, the bill does not in any way put concessionaires in a worse position than public health institutions, but rather puts all potential concessionaires in the same position as to be able to obtain concessions, nor will those with better connections. Nor does the new law abolish existing concessions, at least overnight, bring new financial obligations. In practice, from the Health Insurance Office, we can pay as much services as we have available financial resources and under the same conditions, both public institutions and concessionaires receive money. Concessionaires will therefore not be placed in any worse position than public health institutions by means of a bill, as they will continue to carry out health activities under the same conditions. Both will also be financed under the same conditions as before. The only thing proposed by law is that concessions will be awarded according to the needs of the health needs of the population offered by the existing network of operators for a certain period of time, on the basis of tenders, to all under the same conditions. The novelty that we are considering today therefore brings the following solutions: a single regulation of the authorisations for the exercise of health care activities, a legal void established in the current law, because there are no specific conditions for the procedure for granting authorisations for the exercise of health activities; an arrangement of the institution responsible for carrying out health activities and its full-time or short-term employment; but we would like to point out that, in the following legislative procedure, we will seek solutions towards the elimination of any negative consequences for concessionaires operating services in the public health network; a regulation of the legal basis for carrying out health activities for public welfare institutions and public education institutions; an arrangement of concessions for the conduct of health activities; and the implementation of Directives relating to the work of health workers outside health institutions; an advertising of health activities; supervision of healthcare providers and of healthcare professionals and workers; and the implementation of Directives. We will explain more about this at this point and we will discuss concrete solutions in the debate. We must also stress that the novelty in the transitional and final provisions finally brings solutions to the status of nurses, which is very important for our Group of Members and was also one of the key moments in providing our support for this law. Therefore, the novelty is a step in the right direction, but, as we see, despite the long-term harmonisation, we will try to improve the law in view of the considerable number of comments that it will be able to implement in practice. In our parliamentary group, we will support the proposal that the law is suitable for further consideration.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u60	Thank you for your word. I'll be glad to hear from you. Otherwise, I must conclude that the Health Act has already been written and coordinated in past governments, how very difficult it is to get support from both policies and other stakeholders, shows the result itself. Indeed, no government has been able to do so far, nor has any minister or minister been able to do so. And although it is a systemic law that should have priority in some reform, it has not changed seriously since independence, despite the fact that we all speak together and write about how very poorly certain areas in the law have been regulated. Let us see that we will not just be talking about foreign matters, but what are the goals of the law that is before us today. One of the objectives is to maintain and strengthen a public health system. Health services must not be marketable goods, so there is a public and non-profit activity at the forefront and, of course, a private one can or even must complement it. The granting of concessions in a transparent manner and on the basis of the needs identified, this is what is enshrined in the law and ensuring the quality and expertise of healthcare activities. Which of these objectives is at issue? Of course, many comments have come to the bill and many have also been taken into account, not because we need to follow our vision and, above all, the coalition agreement, in which we have stated that we promise to correct the existing health system in the light of the strengthening of public health and a clearer separation of public and private activities. So we're just doing what we predicted and what we promised. Of course, I understand many objections to this law and the general opposition to all changes in health care. However, the fact that it is necessary to make a decision is to draw a line and, in fact, to do something not to leave everything on purpose and desire. I also read the Court of Auditors audit report on the award of concessions for the period 2000 to 2006. At that time, the Court of Auditors delivered a negative opinion to the Ministry of Health, finding that it did not define the relevant standards, personnel, equipment and material, that it did not produce and prescribe criteria and guidelines for the establishment of a network of public health activities at primary level, in order not to ensure the implementation of all the forms of control provided by the Health Act, so as not to specify in more detail the course of the issue of consent or the disagreement with regard to the award of the concession and much more. In short, it did not provide adequate grounds and established appropriate processes to ensure the functioning of the system of awarding concessions in basic health activities. Let me point out once again, all these findings related to the period 2000-2006. At the start of our government in 2014, the situation was still so, it was not saved. So much for the complexity of this problem and so much for the success or failure of the Minister of Health's work, which has succeeded in bringing the amendments to the national constituency. Of course, this period involved many different governments, including the government of Janez Janša. At the time of their government, the most courageously exploited this loose legislation was their Minister, Mr Bručan, and he suddenly signed 50 new concession contracts in a so-called night action. Of course, this is not a lonely case. I only mentioned this because I wanted to show how many governments and how many ministers have been stumbled by this health legislation for such or other reasons. That's why all the criticisms coming from the right side are the least ridiculous. Rather than constructively dealing with amendments, they deal with their many recommendations, which are even unconstitutional, socially destructive and focused exclusively on their own promotion. Most importantly, because of such past policies and today's opposition approach to problems, no one will live better, and at least patients. Let us focus on the current proposal, which, after long and difficult coordination, we have only received on the table. The main solutions to the proposal before us today are the uniform arrangements for authorisations to carry out health activities, the regulation of legal grounds for carrying out health activities for public welfare institutions and public educational institutions, the arrangements for concessions for the provision of health services, the addition of the arrangements for the work of healthcare professionals outside health institutions, the advertising of health activities and the supervision of healthcare providers and healthcare professionals and employees. The law thus primarily lays down the conditions for granting authorisations for the exercise of a healthcare activity, uniform for all health care providers, irrespective of their status. This eliminates the subordination and weakness of the current regime and removes the legal void in the area of the granting of authorisations to carry out health activities. By laying down the conditions and procedures for the authorisation and the precise conditions for the withdrawal of licences, all operators will be treated equally for the first time. Now I'm going to focus on concessions because they kept lifting, and still, so much dust. The law currently in force regulates this area. We've heard that a number of times. As a result, there are more questions in this area than answers. Above all, there are many unanimity practices. The award of concessions was unsupervised and untransparent. All of this has led to a mix of public and private activities and interests, mainly to the detriment of public health activities. The proposed solution to the amendment of the law states that concessions shall be granted only if the public health service does not ensure the exercise of health activities to the extent determined by the public health service network or if the public health service cannot ensure the necessary accessibility of health services. Concessions will thus be distributed on the basis of the needs expressed, thereby ensuring sufficient accessibility of health services. The concession will be awarded on the basis of a call for tender and the mutual relationship will be governed by a concession contract. However, the condition for the conclusion of this contract will be the prior conclusion of a contract between the concessionaire and the public health institution. There are also specific cases where the concession contract and the conditions for withdrawal are terminated. Concessions will be granted for a certain period of fifteen years and will be extended for a further fifteen years if necessary. Thus, the bill follows the objectives of strengthening public health. A system shall be established to allow for the award of concessions in a transparent manner and strategic planning shall also be required at both primary and secondary levels. Of course, it is also very important that supervision is not defined by the current law, despite the fact that internal professional supervision is prescribed as mandatory. All of this is more than left to individual healthcare providers, and the fundamental purpose of monitoring is difficult to achieve due to inadequate and inadequate regulation. The proposed solution therefore now regulates the area in an integrated manner. The Ministry followed the opinion of the Court of Auditors in regulating this content and identified both inspection bodies and possible statements of measures in the draft law. The Carrier Inspection Authority is the Health Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia. I will not touch all the content that the law changes or regulates, but I would nevertheless like to say that the list of stakeholders who have forwarded their responses is very long. Of course, all of them were somehow united in their view that reform of health legislation was necessary, but sometimes they had a diametric opposite view of what this reform should look like and what it should be like. Since opinions differ so much and are as contradictory as they can be seen today in the National Assembly, all these opinions cannot be taken into account in the preparation of the proposal that we are considering. Of course, it is possible to follow the Resolution on the National Health Plan from 2016 to 2025 “Common for the Society of Health”, which we adopted in this National Assembly. It states that concessions have complemented the activities of health institutions, and I think that we will certainly improve them further by adopting an amendment. But of course, we stand in different positions from the beginning, and I think that the coalition is united and has also written this in a coalition contract, what is our view of the adoption of health legislation? We follow this despite the fact that, of course, you have different views and desires, but this together simply cannot be reconciled and reached with such compromises as to bring diametrically different views into one marriage.

We are talking about the implementation of health-care activities by social welfare institutions, such as nursing homes, special welfare institutions, social welfare institutions for training, welfare centres for home care and work centres, so say the centres where rehabilitation is carried out after brain injuries. All these establishments are currently carrying out a medical activity without authorisation, as well as without a health concession or other legal act which would legally allow them to carry out health activities in the public health service network. This area is governed by the amendment of the law. The amendment also specifies the conditions for obtaining the concession. A legal obligation is laid down to grant the concession on the basis of a tender signed in accordance with the concession act. The conditions to be met by the concessionaire in order to compete on the file will be so publicly published and known in advance. In this way, a number of interested parties will be able to apply for individual concessions, which is certainly a step forward towards transparency and the equalisation of all interested parties in the process of obtaining or applying for concessions. The conditions for the conclusion of the concession contract will be the prior conclusion of a contract between the concessionaire and the public health authority on the scope and types of cooperation for the purposes of the implementation of continuous healthcare and a contract on the insurance of liability for doctors in accordance with the law governing the medical service. The concession contract will also set out cases where the concession contract and the conditions for withdrawal of concessions are terminated. I believe that this will make it much better to obtain or grant concessions than it has been so far. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u84	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Minister, say good-bye again! We're gonna have to hold on a little longer. Colleagues and colleagues! I will now read the Committee's report, given that in a few minutes I will be back behind this speaker panel when I present to you the position of our Group of Members, and now I will present the report from the Committee's meeting. The Health Committee shall meet at its 23rd session, on 10. 5. 2017 as a parent working body, the proposal for a recommendation on the issue of Slovenian health, submitted to the National Assembly for consideration by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed Jozet Tank. The proposal for a recommendation was published on the website of the National Assembly, in the Rapporteur of 29. 3. 2017. No amendments were tabled within the time limit set by the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. The introductory supplementary explanation of the proposal was given by Jelko Godec on behalf of the applicant. She pointed out that there were many problems in Slovenian health care that were not being solved. The National Assembly therefore recommends that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia and the President of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia immediately suspend all those responsible in the health sector who have not prevented or even allowed corruption and crime in procurement, and those who have not amended health laws and acts in a timely manner. The Minister of Health is subjectively and objectively responsible for underestimating the health situation and should therefore resign and otherwise resign the President of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. In the area, the proposer highlighted corruption in health care, joint public procurement, long waiting periods, irregularities in the recruitment of individual public health institutions and at the Ministry of Health, and losses in public health institutions. The representative of the Ministry of Health, the Secretary of State, strongly opposed the allegations that the situation in Slovenian health is as catastrophic as the proposaler is trying to present the recommendation. She then presented the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia and provided explanations as well as explanations concerning the above-mentioned content in specific areas. In particular, she stressed that all the comments made by the proposal's proponents were misleading, since the material's proponents deliberately gave the impression that they were exceptional in the field of health. The Ministry of Health does not underestimate the importance of the financial, waiting and operating situation of public health institutions, which is reflected in the legislative changes made and in concrete measures that provide conditions for the transparent, efficient and quality functioning of the health system in the long term. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia rejects all allegations that the conduct of the Minister of Health or the President of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is inadequate, since the legality and the measures being implemented are carried out professionally and legally. The Committee took note of the written opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service. She pointed out that the first priority recommendation proposed the identification of the responsibility of the Minister of Health with regard to the conduct and the health situation, which are detailed in the explanation of the recommendation. The responsibility of the Minister of Health of the National Assembly can undoubtedly be discussed and decided on, but it is intended to do so by the Constitutional Institute of Interpellation, which is one of the basic institutes of the relationship of division of power between legislative and executive and is governed by Articles 118 of the Constitution and 250 to 253 of the Rules of Procedure. This institution allows at least 10 Members to intercede with regard to the work of each Minister; on the other hand, it allows the Minister to prepare an answer to the interpretation and provides him with the appropriate time. Since the recommendation deals with issues which, by content and by purpose, may only be subject to interpretation, is not consistent with the Constitution and the Rules of Procedure and is also not submitted in the form, manner and procedure laid down in the Rules of Procedure, it is therefore contrary to the principle of the division of power. In line with the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, the Coalition Members have proposed that a decision be put to the vote that the recommendation is not appropriate for further consideration. In accordance with the second paragraph of Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted and adopted the following decision: The proposal for a recommendation on the issue of Slovenian health is not suitable for further consideration.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u87	I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Health, Dr. Ana Medved, for the opening presentation of the position on the proposal of the parent working body. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u88	Thank you. Good afternoon, everyone! The Ministry of Health shall carry out measures and activities to ensure, in the long term, the conditions for the transparent, efficient and quality functioning of the healthcare system. With regard to the issue of Slovenian health care regarding corruption in health care, recruitment in public health institutions, the adoption of legislation, waiting periods, as well as the indebtedness or poor operation of public health institutions, we provide the following clarifications. The issue of procurement of medicines and medical devices in hospitals and related corruption risks or the existence of corruption has been present in Slovenia for a longer period, and the Ministry of Health, during the term of office of this Government, has approached the problem on the basis of a clearly defined and reported position and the objective of zero tolerance for corruption. The Ministry of Health carries out several activities in this field. Thus, with the aim of preventing overpayments, the Ministry of Health made recommendations to all public health institutions regarding the implementation of public procurement procedures; the Ministry of Public Administration was involved in the common procurement procedures; it also supported the Association of Health Institutions of Slovenia to establish a common base of medicines called Intravisor. Hospitals should use that base as one of the basis for market verification in public procurement procedures. Data on the prices of materials are primarily used to assist health-care directors and other responsible persons to purchase in responsible and rational decision-making regarding the procurement of material, to develop the estimated value of each contract; at the same time, they are also a good bargaining point for the contracting authority in the implementation of the new procurement procedures. The application currently covers data from all hospitals and three health homes and will gradually add data from other health homes. With regard to recruitment in public health institutions, we reiterate that the appointment of representatives of the founder is carried out in accordance with the Protocol on the selection of candidates for representatives of the founder in the Councils of Public Health Institutions, where the founder of the Republic of Slovenia is. Here, for the first time in 2015, clear criteria for the appointment of representatives of the founder were laid down on the basis of public calls. The Ministry of Health has also prepared material for the work of representatives to be more professional and effective for the work of representatives of the founder in the Councils of Public Health Institutions, the founder of which is the Republic of Slovenia. This material contains the most important guidelines and is also made publicly available on the websites of the Ministry of Health. The Ministry of Health also organises consultations with representatives at least twice a year because the government does not directly manage representatives. Since the government does not exercise direct management in public health institutions, but has transferred this function to its representatives in the world of the institution, the saints of the institutions in the appointment of directors are completely independent, and their consent to the government. With regard to the adoption of legislation, we explain that the amendment of the Law on Health Activities in the Government was adopted on 21 April 2017, and this month a general debate will take place in the National Assembly, now. We estimate that the amendment will be adopted in advance of parliamentary holidays. With regard to the adoption of the new Health and Health Insurance Act, however, comments are being reviewed, meetings with different stakeholders and the drafting of the complementary text of the bill. As far as waiting times are concerned, the Ministry of Health has taken an active approach to their resolution. In 2016, additional funds have already been provided under the general agreement to reduce waiting periods, similar to or to a greater extent this year. This dedicated funding for the implementation of the one-year project totals €36 million, €18 million in 2017 and €18 million in 2018. In the framework of the partnership negotiations, additional funding of €20 million will be provided for the implementation of the single additional programmes of the so-called EDP with a view to reducing the number of waiting periods. Additional resources to reduce waiting periods will also be provided through the gradual transfer of funding for non-health services, namely tertiary 1 and traineeships and specialisations, from the CFSP to the state budget starting in 2017, when the transfer will amount to EUR 20 million. In order to improve the operating conditions of public health institutions, the government increased the prices of health services in 2015 and 2016, as they have been temporarily reduced several times in the past. These prices increased by 2 % between July and December 2015 and by 3,6 % between June and December 2016. So much for now, thank you.

In this mandate, the Government and the Ministry of Health have approached the problem systematically, systematically, economically and on the basis of a clear and communication position and the objective of zero tolerance to corruption. The planned measures are implemented, are linked to the procurement and management of medical equipment for public health institutions, hospitals and the procurement of medicines and medical devices in hospitals. The key elements for reducing corruption risks, transparent and rational use of public funds and, in this context, the proper implementation of public procurement are: standardisation, central or common procurement and thorough prior market verification. During this term of office, the Government has taken a number of measures that have a direct impact on the flow of healthcare supplies, with the Ministry pointing out: strengthening the responsibility of the hospital procurement management, introducing controls, establishing medical and technical equipment bases and standardising or establishing uniform technical specifications for identical articles. In addition, the implementation of the common procurement for all items of medical and technical devices which prove that this is justified in terms of the purpose of the device, the price, the necessary purchase quantity, the situation on the market and the potential corruption risk. The rules of operation of the ESF state that this is a three-partite body where employers, employees and government representatives are equally represented. The rules on its functioning in Article 18 make it possible for young people, older persons, disabled persons and other stakeholders to be present at meetings where important intergenerational issues are addressed. With this change, the government has therefore sought a solution so that young people can participate in meetings on matters that are most affected, and so is the case. In particular, it should be stressed that the ESF is, by its very nature, a space for industrial relations and that social partnerships between different social groups can take place in a variety of other ways. This allows young people to defend their interest within the framework of the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Youth, which is responsible for the field of youth or for the realization of public interest in the youth sector at national level. In addition, young people can participate in policy-making within a number of youth organisations – the Youth Council of Slovenia, the Student Organization of Slovenia, the Diagnostic Organization of Slovenia and so on. In this case, however, it is necessary to consider a different solution or cooperation and not necessarily the involvement of civil society through full or associated membership in the Economic and Social Council. In this regard, we stress, as also pointed out by the Legislative and Legal Service, that most of the social partners are not organisations and individuals who perform public services or exercise public powers, to which, in accordance with Article 111 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the National Assembly may at all propose measures for work. The enforcement procedure itself expects a type of debtor's activity to avoid a creditor's proposal and not to be thrown on the road. But what if these expected activities are in many cases not on the part of the debtor himself. People on the verge of despair do not find themselves in a multitude of legal regulations. In view of the above, it is necessary to seek an additional path by which, in addition to the measures it has taken, for example, as part of the debt write-off package, the State could further help to eliminate the unloved, sad business stories. But it's moving. Let me mention that in preparation for the amendment of the Law on Enforcement and Insurance – in fact, we have already received the coalition MPs to deal with – which is supposed to solve the problems we have witnessed in recent months. The option proposed by the Ministry of Justice will, of course, be welcome for a debtor who is not alone, who needs not only material assistance, but in particular someone who will help him find a solution to his problems. Of course, this cannot be done by a court, but it can and must also be done by social work centres within the framework of their basic mission. More involvement is expected mainly in solving the housing problem of economically weaker citizens and young families. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u170	Thank you for your word. I almost thought the government would surprise me, but unfortunately it was just a mistake. The amendment to the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, which we have prepared in Novi Slovenije and which we have already submitted to parliamentary treatment for the second time, brings two key innovations. Both will significantly improve the business environment and provide more jobs. For the first time, the amendment allows double status for retired independent entrepreneurs, and for the second time, the law limits the reimbursement to the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance of Slovenia due to the consequences of accidents at work. First, a few words about double status. Many citizens who qualify for retirement want to retire and for various reasons continue to work. The activities they have carried out so far also want to be carried out in the future. There are also those who want to open espas and provide other services at a time when they are retired. The current Slovenian legislation provides that pensioners must decide whether to take the pension they deserve and close their espas or to give up a certain part of their pension in order to continue their activities. In New Slovenia, we feel that in this way we needlessly restrict citizens, especially pensioners who still want to work, maintain their business and jobs or work to improve their social situation. With our proposal for amendments to these independent entrepreneurs, we allow them to receive a full, well-deserved, 100 % old pension while opening espies at the same time, paying full contributions for full-time work. To make the proposal in the right direction, we are also encouraged by the Government's opinion in the White Paper on pensions, which clearly states that this is one of the solutions to be implemented in the future. The imposition of obstacles to the non-implementation of dual status is harmful for citizens and budget revenues, as is also the case with specific data, which, only in January and February 2016, after the last modification of the regime and the definitive abolition of the possibility of double status for certain categories of pensioners in the basic register, deleted a thousand 942 esps. According to the local authorities, when espians had the opportunity to continue their activities while at the same time receiving only 20% of their pension, the majority decided on a second option, namely that they had closed their activities or transferred them to another person and received a hundred percent pension. In some ways, the country has lost a thousand,942 individuals who have paid full contributions or at least part of their contributions to the pension and health allowance. The second point that we are amending in this law is the limitation of the reimbursements that the pension and disability insurance institution may require from employers due to the consequences of accidents at work. Such a proposal has already been tabled several times by Nova Slovenija, unfortunately we have failed because you do not want to understand the essence and the point of our proposal. In this attempt, we have further elaborated this law, drank it even more clearly, and wrote very clearly that when an employer causes an accident, an accident on purpose, I stress, on purpose, it must take over the costs of all pensions and treatments in its entirety. However, if these accidents are due to unfortunate circumstances, we place a 20-fold limit on the minimum pension base for both legal and natural persons, which means nominally around €15 000. With the support of this law, we will take a major step forward towards improving the business environment and more jobs, so I invite you to support this change.

Tonino, amendments are proposed with regard to the so-called dual pensioner status and changes with regard to the employer's liability rule to the pension insurance institution in the event that the insured person suffers damage and, on the basis of this, enforces rights under the general rules of insurance. I would like to point out in the opening, however, because it is also important for the debate that both the double status of independent entrepreneurs and the issue of the compensation claims of the institution to employers are subject to a constitutional judgment which has not yet been decided by the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia to date in this part. Nevertheless, even in the event of a possible change of legislation before the end of the proceedings before the Constitutional Court, changes to the so-called dual status of pensioners should be made with discretion and arrangements should be in place that are identical to those of all insured persons. With regard to the issue of so-called regression claims, however, we explain that the proposal proposes amendments 190, 191 and 193 of the current law, underlining the unlimited disproportionate liability of employers for the damage to be caused by changes in compensation liability. The proposed legislation states that, by amending the law, they wish to impose the employer's liability for damage only if the damage is caused on purpose or because of gross negligence. It should be pointed out that, in accordance with Article 191, employers are responsible for failing to carry out safety and health measures at work or other measures which are prescribed or designed for human safety, even if they have concluded a working relationship without prior medical examination with a person. Not only is this because, as a result of this, all other insured persons are not liable disproportionately to the damage to the worker we cover if you want to, because in all cases of pension and disability contributions, all other insured persons are liable, in no case shall employers be liable to disproportionately, without limitation to any minimum error, since in all cases the identification and enforcement of compensation is determined whether all claims of compensation are made under the general rules of civil law, including misconduct, causation between harmful conduct and injury, but also taking into account shared liability when deciding on the amount of compensation. For these reasons, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not support the proposal of the law. We believe that the proposal of the law opposes the rules of general indemnity, opposes the rules of safety and health at work that we must all work towards together, and we consider the proposed dual status regime only for independent entrepreneurs to be lacking, and above all, it is contrary to the principle of equality. The government does not support the bill. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u173	We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. Mr Marijan Pojbič, the Slovenian Democratic Party's parliamentary group, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u174	Your Honor! We have before us a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Pension and Disability Insurance, which was tabled by the NSI Group for consideration in the National Assembly. The law proposes two solutions. Firstly, the proposal to regulate the dual status of insured persons for pension and disability insurance in such a way that each insured person has the opportunity to continue his or her activity and receive a full old-age pension when he fulfils the conditions for it, or when he or she receives a new pension, without the already acquired pension being reduced. Secondly, it is proposed that the employer's liability, as determined by the Slovenian Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance, would be acceptable if the employer violates the rules on purpose or because of gross negligence. However, in the event that the injury is caused by the worker, or in the event of minor negligence, it is not. The law will be supported by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group in its first reading for the following reasons. We agree that the pension is a right to be acquired when the conditions are met on the basis of the contributions paid. It follows that everyone, irrespective of the activity he carries out, has the right to a pension when he fulfils the conditions for doing so. Where the pensioner continues to carry out activities, this means only additional payments to the pension and disability allowance and additional value for the social product. Many pension recipients do not choose to carry out their activities or do activities in black because their pensions would have been reduced disproportionately depending on how much work they would have to do in order to make up for their pension. The current regime also provides for the possibility of abuse of the right to a partial pension, since the payment of a partial pension depends on the amount of hours paid and not on the actual payment to carry out the activity. In our view, the proposed solution deserves further legislative treatment, in particular to verify its financial impact, which, according to experience from other EU countries, should even be positive. However, the second solution proposed by this law has been dealt with several times in the National Assembly and has not been sufficiently supportive. The aim is to relieve the employer of the regression claim by the Slovenian Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance in the event of damage to the worker due to the minor negligence of the employer. We are fully aware of the fact that the expansion of the liability of compensation affects mainly smaller craftsmen and smaller private employers, for whom such a regression requirement of the institution can also mean the closure of the activity. The solution is partly acceptable to us, but only if it is clearly determined, on the other hand, that the disabled or other insurance worker will in any event be compensated for the damage caused by the minor negligence of the employer in the injury to the work process. Further to the legislative process, better solutions could also be sought in cooperation with the social partners, namely the solution that the worker would not be deprived in any way of the negligence of the employer, and not because of the minor mistakes that occur in each work process. I call again on the other parliamentary groups to support the further legislative process and we are working together to find solutions that will be acceptable to all; workers, employers and those who wish to continue their activities even after meeting the conditions for an old pension.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u181	The position of the Nova Slovenija Group – Christian Democrats will be moved by Mrs Iva Dimic.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u182	Dear colleagues and colleagues! In our core programme, Christian Democrats advocate the inclusion of European standards in our labour law regarding the provision of working conditions, working hours, leave, protection at work, conditions of employment or termination of employment. However, working conditions that go beyond these standards must be addressed in social dialogue between employers and workers or their associations or unions. The State must invest more resources in promoting health at work, raising awareness and informing employers and workers. This should result in fewer accidents and consequently less costs for the treatment or payment of pension and disability benefits. In Novi Slovenija we support the amendment of the law on pension and disability insurance, which we have re-invested in parliamentary treatment. The key solutions presented by the novel are the following. First time. Regulation of the scope of the Regression Applications of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office, so that the employer is liable only if he violates the rules on safety and health at work on purpose or because of gross negligence. When the cause of injury on the part of the worker or in cases of minor negligence, it is not. Another time. The legal limitation of the amount of compensation, on a maximum of 20 times the lowest pension base, except in the case of a premeditation. Third time. Arrangements for double status. The rescheduling of the pension and disability insurance institution to employers is regulated by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, ZPIZ-2. The regulation is similar to the regulation of the regression claims of the Institute for Health Insurance of Slovenia to employers. We have already discussed them, and the proposal of New Slovenia was unfortunately not adopted. The problem in the area of excessive and unpredictable regression claims has not been the case since yesterday. Traders and small entrepreneurs have been alerting them for years. The fact is that the current legislation and practice are such that a specific employer can have well-ordered internal acts, rules and education of workers in the field of safety and health at work, and workers can be well-educated in all these rules, and this may be due to non-compliance with instructions, non-use of safety equipment or other work accidents. And in the worst case case, a small employer, who is generally also economically weaker, may face such a high regressive requirement by the Institute of Pension and Disability Insurance that his existence is threatened. Let us note in particular that in the event of a closure of trades or businesses, all, both the employer and the worker, are out of work and income. In the case of independent entrepreneurs who respond with all their assets, whole families may be at risk. The second essential reason for supporting the adoption of the law by New Slovenia is the fact that, by limiting the regressive claims to a maximum of 20 times the minimum pension base, the basis for the creation of insurance products by commercial insurance companies will be regulated. Currently, craftsmen and entrepreneurs cannot insure the risk of regressive claims in commercial insurance companies. As for the dual status arrangement. Christian Democrats believe that the current solution is criminal, creating inequalities before the law. In our view, each insured person must be able to continue or establish his or her activity, while receiving the full retirement pension to which he or she is entitled. Why would the state make it impossible to work for someone who would still want to work after retirement? We estimate that this can only be good for the pension fund, as the full contributions of these insurers will be an additional inflow, which the state has given up with the government novelty. We also estimate that such a way of resolving dual status is more appropriate than the one recently introduced by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, as it encourages insured persons who would like to remain active even after meeting the conditions for an old or early retirement to do so legally. Members of New Slovenia will support the proposal for a new law on pension and disability insurance.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u183	Mr Saša Tabakovic will present the position of the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u184	Dear Presidents, colleagues and colleagues! The solutions envisaged in today's amendment to the Pension and Disability Insurance Act have already been discussed in the National Assembly last year. Novela is referring to two solutions. The first solution concerns the regulation of the dual status of insurers or independent entrepreneurs in a way that allows for the simultaneous receipt of the entire pension and the maintenance of independent activities to an unlimited extent. The second solution, however, refers to the regression claims of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office relating to the employer's liability when there is damage at work or occupational illness due to non-compliance with the safety and health rules at work. The Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance may require compensation for damage caused by disability, physical impairment, the need for foreign assistance and service or the death of an insured person on purpose or because of negligence. In this case, the novice suggests that the employer's liability should be excluded if it is a matter of ease and that the amount of compensation should be reduced to 20 times the lowest pension base. Restricting compensation upwards following a new proposal from the law would no longer be possible when the employer acted on purpose. It is also proposed to limit the employer's liability for the worker when the damage occurred intentionally and in this case it is obliged to settle the cause. The proposed solutions relating to the dual status of independent entrepreneurs are considered to be comparable to those in other EU countries. Here again, we find that EU members have different arrangements for this area. In some cases, dual status is limited in some way by profit from the pursuit of an activity or additional earnings when receiving a pension at the same time, in the case of Germany, or by any new employment contract, in the case of Poland, and there is no difference in the treatment of independent entrepreneurs and workers in the labour relationship, in the case of Austria. In this regard, it should also be pointed out that the possibility of maintaining a separate activity while receiving a partial pension or payment of a 20% early or old pension is pending before the Constitutional Court and, for this reason, amending pension legislation in the work relating to dual status is not appropriate in our view. However, in the case of regressive claims, we reiterate that the current pension and disability insurance arrangements include both liability for the damage caused by gross negligence and general negligence. The format of the fault is determined in accordance with Article 135 of the Bond Code. However, the cause of the injury is required to demonstrate that it acted with special care in its handling of the injury. It should also be pointed out that the employer does not respond when the full liability is on the employee’s side after the applicable scheme, since the pre-condition for the employer’s liability is also a causal link between the illegal conduct or omission and the occurrence of injury. Finally, let us point out once again that expenditure from the pension and disability allowance which is not covered by the payments is covered by the State budget. Responsibilities and the proposed limitation on the amount of compensation up so far represent a burden on other insurers. We will not support this bill in the Modern Centre Party Group because of that proposal. Thank you very much.
