ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u200	Dear colleagues, colleagues, representative of the Ministry, good evening! When at the beginning of this year, through social networks, I asked young people about the problems they face and what bothers them in particular, I also ran into a group of students from the University of Primorska, in the direction of Biopsychology. On the basis of several meetings and meetings, it was clear to me that when these students entered the study course from 2010 onwards, the 2010–2011 study year, they were found to have been misled by the State in their employment opportunities and professional opportunities, as it has not yet regulated legislation, the fields of ZOFVI and social protection. The main objective of the proposed amendment of the law is to include the students of the study programmes Biopsychology among the recruitment of professional workers under Article 69 of the Social Protection Act. The State plays a decisive role in the process of setting up and accrediting study programmes and is consequently legally required to do everything necessary and reasonably expected of the country to personally experience the social need and professional importance of the completed study programme, at least with an equal opportunity to apply effectively to the jobs applied for and resulting employment. By amending Article 69 of the Social Protection Act, graduates of biopsychology are allowed to carry out professional examinations and consequently have the same opportunity to enter the labour market where they can verify, prove and upgrade professional competences. The proposed law has no financial implications for the state budget or other public funds. The programme follows and includes the starting points and the development of scientific knowledge in the United States of America, the United Kingdom, Germany and Italy. This particularity and added value of the study programme Biopsychology offers an opportunity and a challenge for students of psychological science at home and abroad, as it offers and complements conventional methods of work and research. It is also worth adding that Biopsychology is a public study programme at a public institution, the University of Primorska. It is only an exercise of equal opportunities in the employment of biopsychologists in the field of social protection, and this is not only about the possibility of working in the public sector, but also about working in non-governmental organisations, such as Sweet or the Red Cross, since the status of an expert in the field of social protection is often a condition for applying for different calls. It is mainly European here. This law also aims to harmonise the actual situation, since biopsychologists are already on the labour market and the formal legal one that is set up at the time of the establishment of the study course Biopsychology 2010. The Ministry has also pointed out in its position that the inclusion of biopsychologists in this area is an added value, and some other directions are likely to be added to the list in the future. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u211	Thank you very much. Mag. Tanja Zink will present the position of the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u212	Dear Vice President, colleagues and colleagues! The current Social Protection Act lays down, in Article 69, the profiles of professional workers who can provide social protection services, but among which there are no graduates of the study course Biopsychology. Biopsychology is an interdisciplinary science that combines science and society. The proposed amendment proposes that graduates of biopsychology be allowed to carry out professional examinations and consequently equal opportunities for entry into the labour market of regulated professions. As stated in the explanatory memorandum, the involvement of biopsychologists in the recruitment of professional workers would contribute to the quality of the provision of social security services. In 2009, the Faculty of Mathematics, Science and Information Technology of the University of Primorska acquired accreditation for the postgraduate study programme Biopsychology and in 2013 for the second degree study programme, promising future graduates that they would be able to work in the public sector after completion of the study, particularly in health, education, social protection and public administration. Since the profile of biopsychologists is not included in the recruitment of professional workers under the current Social Protection Act, graduates of this study course cannot be employed in the field of social protection. Of course, this raises the question of whose competence it is to check employment opportunities. Biopsychology students have pointed out that the National Agency for Quality in Higher Education should in this case be a protector if a study programme is accredited that does not allow the employability of graduates. It should be noted that at the time when the study programmes of Biopsychology were accredited in the criteria for accreditation in relation to employability, it was only stated that the need for the first and second stages of study programmes was also assessed as regards the employability of graduates and the possibility of further education. In May 2014, new accreditation criteria were adopted, where, with regard to the employment of graduates, the planning of the higher education institution is also an integral part of the accreditation process in terms of the quality of learning outcomes and competences in the way that graduates are employed or can continue to be educated. The higher education institution must also monitor the employment or employment of its graduates. Although the new accreditation criteria currently in force, which impose more responsibility on the higher education institution to verify the employability of graduates of selected study programmes, we estimate that, at the time of accreditation of the study programmes of the Biopsychology of NAKVIS, he performed his work superficially. In order to avoid similar, unloving situations in which graduates are found after the completion of the study, last year's amendment to the Higher Education Act in Article 32 clearly states that the NAKVIS for regulated professions in the accreditation process must obtain the consent of the Ministry in which the profession acquired by the diplomat belongs. The applicant submits that on the basis of the standard classification of professions, it can be concluded that the profession of a biopsychologist is comparable to that of a psychologist. This raises the question of the appropriateness and comparability of the competences acquired by biopsychologists in the study process. It should be stressed that in the field of education, biopsychologists are accredited as biologists. The Department of Psychology of the Faculty of Philosophy also pointed out the lack of adopted social skills of biologists. In doing so, we cannot ignore the fact that the proposed law only regulates this problem for biopsychologists, although there are other profiles that may be employed in the field of social protection. We also need to ask ourselves who should be responsible for assessing the professionality of profiles and the ability to carry out regulated professions. In this case, the responsibility which has not been taken over by the Faculty in the planning of the study programme and then by the NAKVIS in the accreditation of the programme is actually transferred to the National Assembly. Members of the Modern Centre Party will decide on the support of the bill at their own discretion. Thank you.

For New Slovenia, however, the most controversial part of the proposal is the introduction of a moratorium on the land on which Roma reside. In practice, following the applicant's claim, it has already happened several times that the land on which Roma live has been sold. As a result, the Roma had to move away, which would cause even more problems than the original ones. In Novi Slovenija, we cannot agree that we could define as controversial the ownership of the owner with our property and even bind the owner with our hands. In such cases, the problem is not at all in the owner's possession of ownership rights, but in the fact that a certain group of people live on land that is not really theirs. In our view, issues should be addressed where there is a problem, not by a way of dealing with consequences that would unduly affect third parties. The proposed solution is therefore, in our view, contrary to the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. At the same time, we Christian Democrats also note that in Slovenia, unfortunately, there are some problems with understanding the concept of property rights, which has not been shown recently in the adoption of the so-called lex Magna Act. For these reasons, we will vote in the New Slovenia Group against the proposed amendment to the Law on the Roma Community in the Republic of Slovenia.

In our country, only the Italian and Hungarian national communities have constitutional and legally recognized status as indigenous national communities. The Roma community does not have the status of a classical national minority, but it is a special community with its anthropological, ethnic and cultural characteristics. However, the Constitution provides for a special situation and special rights governed by the law. That is why we are all committed to this community being recognised and given special rights in a way that will improve their situation and enable them to participate more effectively in society, including or, in particular, in the field of education and employment. Since the basic law on the Roma community has been adopted since long ago 2007, we are therefore aware of the fact that the need for its amendment is being demonstrated. These have already been prepared several times today, but none of them has been aligned with the professional and interested public, and the solutions proposed have been too incomplete or too partial to be supported. Finally, two years ago a law was submitted to the National Assembly with a largely similar content, which was not supported for those reasons. There are questionable or problematic solutions to the moratorium on private property intervention and the possible non-conformity with the Constitution. That is why we have no choice but to reject the proposed law. The supply of drinking water is also currently in accordance with the applicable legislation and must be regulated for all social groups in the same manner, irrespective of any personal circumstances. It is not currently possible to connect illegal facilities to a public electricity network in accordance with the provisions of the Law on the Construction of Buildings and Energy Law. The modern centre and the government are aware of the importance of regulating the open issues of the Roma community and addressing the identified shortcomings of the current regulation, and we have therefore called on the Government to prepare as soon as possible a proposal for amendments and additions to the Law on the Roma Community in the context of the expert discussion on inter-sectoral coordination and coordination with the Roma community. The Office of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia for Nationality has already joined the drafting of the regulation and is currently in the stage of drafting the working text of amendments and supplementing the law. Furthermore, in early May, the Government set up an Inter-Resort Working Group to address the spatial problem of certain members of the Roma community, which is responsible for reviewing the situation of individual Roma settlements in Slovenia, preparing an analysis of good practices in this area, and proposing measures to address spatial issues and improve their living conditions. This does not mean that we are not seeking to protect the Roma community in Slovenia or to provide conditions for the preservation and development of its culture, language and tradition, the regulation of the living conditions, adequate education and participation in economic, cultural and social life.

In Slovenia, we do not know how many Roma live or how many live in individual municipalities. We don't know how many illegal settlements there are. We don't know how many Roma are employed. We have no data on how many Roma complete primary education. And we don't even know what the percentage of Roma children coming to school is. We are giving out various forms of social assistance, but we do not know what this aid is being used for. We do not even have data on breaches of public order and peace in the area where the Roma population lives, although the situation in some environments is already so problematic that citizens and citizens who pay taxes to that country, who respect the laws and rules, are forced to sell and move out of their homes, even though they have been difficult to build and with great denial. The same applies to Roma who want to live in accordance with the country's standards. If all of this country is not addressed as a priority, no new legislation and no strategy will help. Only the dissatisfaction between the Roma and the rest of the population will increase.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u235	Thank you very much. Mr Peter Wilphan will present the position of the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners of Slovenia. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u236	Dear Presidents, colleagues and colleagues! In this term of office of the National Assembly, for the second time, we have a proposal for a law on Parliamentary benches trying to solve the problems they face in many Roma settlements. The current Law on the Roma Community in the Republic of Slovenia was adopted in 2007 and is based on the provision of Article 65 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia that the situation and special rights of the Roma community living in Slovenia are governed by law. While this law provides a framework for regulating the situation of the Roma community in Slovenia, it lays down the concerns of the state authorities and the authorities of the local self-government in the exercise of the specific rights of the Roma community, regulates the organisation of the Roma community at national and local level and funding, but in practice there have been major difficulties in the implementation of the law, as the Ombudsman has repeatedly pointed out in his annual report and has been constantly alerted to by the organisations of the Roma communities. It is not to be overlooked that the Ministry responsible for infrastructure and space has also given warnings due to the inadequacy of Article 5 of the current law. They pointed out that the instrument of State control of the municipalities in regulating illegal and municipally unregulated Roma settlements was unclear or even impracticable. We cannot and do not wish to deny the proposalers' findings that there have also been some shortcomings in the functioning of the Council of the Roma Community of the Republic of Slovenia in practice, which is why we should consider the possibilities for different design and composition of this organisation. However, unfortunately, in the Group of Members, we agree with the Government's view that there is a need for serious reflection on many provisions and key solutions to the current law, which requires a more demanding and in-depth drafting of amendments and supplementing the law than has been done so far. It is not to be forgotten that Roma have been living in the territory of today's Slovenia since 14. For centuries. According to current estimates in Slovenia, between 7,000 and 12 thousand Roma live, representing about 0.5% of the total population. The number can even be higher. The fact is that solving the spatial problem and regulating the housing situation of Roma is a process that can only be successful with the partnership of municipalities, Roma and state institutions, in which each of the actors must fulfil the obligations of their respective competences. Addressing these issues requires inter-departmental cooperation between the competent ministries, municipalities and also the Roma communities. According to the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, it is aware of the importance of regulating open issues and addressing the identified shortcomings of the regulation in force and will prepare itself a proposal for amendments and supplementing the Law on the Roma Community, of course in cooperation with all stakeholders. In the Desus Group, we understand the reasons and purpose of the proposaler for changes to the current law, but we want the Government, as also the Director of the Government of the Nationality Office, to take a comprehensive approach to the amendment of the Law on the Roma Community. According to government representatives, the bill has already been drafted, so we hope or expect the Desus Group to be on our benches in the near future.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u237	Thank you very much. Mag. Bojana Muršič will present the position of the Group of Social Democrats. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u238	Thank you very much, Vice President for your word. Today I started with the views of the parliamentary groups, and after 13 hours I conclude. This amendment to the law that we have on the table proposes solutions that deal with the Roma issue and refer to changes in the allocation of funding to municipalities in which the Roma community lives to the moratorium on the sale of land where Roma live on the composition of the Council of the Roma Community. Similar marriage solutions were also recorded in a novel that we considered in autumn 2015 and assessed that the proposal was not suitable for further consideration. We are social democrats then and again today that the solution to the Roma problem needs to be addressed comprehensively, together with representatives of the Roma community, the local community and the country. Since the regulation of the Roma issue goes beyond the one-resource framework, it is essential to draw up a national Roma action programme for the period 2016-2021. If we have said two years ago that the national programme for the period 2010-2015 will expire at any time, we now find that time is long overdue. The draft national programme of action for Roma for 2016-2021 is ready. However, as we can read, it does not bring satisfactory solutions because these are not properly evaluated. To think about, perhaps we should also discuss extending the period until 2026, especially because the speed of programme preparation and later implementation are not the virtues that would contribute to the development and resolution of this complex problem. In order to increase this speed, however, Roma organisations and their members will also have to contribute. This is also noted by the Ombudsman, who says that by establishing the Council of the Roma Community, part of the competence and responsibility for the resolution of the Roma community is transferred to the members of this community. They must make an urgent effort to include as many children as possible in the educational system in addition to regulating the living conditions. We are aware that this is a long-term process that is ultimately being repaid. In Slovenia we have examples where, on both sides, there is a Roma and non-Roma side, a tendency to regulate, not only living conditions, but also to regulate and develop educational and cultural conditions, very large. It is necessary to build on these positive cases. We will support the proposal that the proposed law is not suitable for further consideration. In view of the final part of the record in the Government's opinion, however, we can kindly request that the preparation of the relevant documents and legislation in this area be completed as soon as possible. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-25-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u239	Thank you very much. We have concluded by presenting the views of the groups of Members. On the proposal to decide that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration, we will decide on Tuesday, 30 May, in the framework of the vote, in accordance with the timing of the meeting of the National Assembly.
